# Seed 28 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - nine hours on four cores (Iteration 106)

`seed 28 tag2 9h then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 10 --cpus 2,8`,
commit 940814b6e (Iteration 105): the first run with four workers (cpus 4,
8, 6 and 12, each pinned with its builds to its own core), the long
workloads (Iteration 100) and the gene rtempty (Iteration 101); population
256, generations until the deadline - which stopped it before generation
159. 39,985 designs, 2.7 s each for a worker; 2,305 refused by the reach
limit, one old-format design that does not convert (cpt16 at scale 3), no
other death. Started 03:42, packed 11:32, the comparison done 12:24 - 8 h
42 min. Session 25 measured it with seeds 24-27, all of them with the long
workloads (calibration 0.993; cpu 2 0.993, cpu 8 0.998; two CPUs: median 1.003, 10-90% 0.991-1.017).

**The stopping rule: not small - by a hair**: the fastest +5.0%, on the bar.

| objective | seed 28's best | before (seeds 24-27, this session) | improvement | bar |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fastest | 0.132 (679fdd08a1) | 0.139 (seed 27) | **+5.0%** | 5% |
| smallest binary + image | 24,874 (eaa2b9e35d) | 24,923 | +0.20% | 2% |
| least memory | 188 KB | 188 KB | none | 8 KB |

Speeds here are in the long workloads' units: not comparable with the
records of seeds before 28 (the comparison measured everything anew).

**The front of all runs: 67** - 66 of seed 28, one of seed 27; 20 by memory
alone. The fastest 679fdd08a1 0.132 at 38,560 bytes; the smallest
eaa2b9e35d 24,874 bytes (0.550). Q (information only, the owner): best
fc52c18d76 (0.151 at 34,464 bytes); the fastest 1.04 times its Q, the
smallest 2.79 times.

**Four workers did not hurt the measurements**: a front design's speed in
the run against its speed measured again alone (6 rounds) - seed 28 median
0.927, 10-90% 0.900-0.955; the single-worker night runs 0.942 (0.896-0.990)
and 0.902 (0.848-0.992). Below 1 in every run - the front collects the
designs that measured lucky - but tighter now: the long workloads.

**Still improving at the end**: 60 of the run's 86 front designs were born
after generation 120; the best recorded speed 0.129 at the start (the
carried fronts), 0.124 by generation 10, 0.121 by 80, 0.119 by 158; the
smallest total 24,923 -> 24,874 by generation 120.

**The genes on the front of all runs (67)**: t2wide 63, opbody 66, hotinl
37 (28 at 10 sites, 6 at 20, 3 at 40), the JIT 18, rtempty 17. On the run's
own front (86): t2wide 82, opbody 84, hotinl 48, the JIT 28, rtempty 27.

## The run's report

# Evolved VM designs

39985 designs evaluated, 37679 alive. Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, parse, corpus, loop,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.
